115 年 8 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
8 月出口 824.0 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 9.4%，較上年
同月增 41.0%(按新臺幣計算增 52.6%)；累計 1 至 8 月出口 5,743.4
億美元，較上年同期增 44.2%(按新臺幣計算增 46.5%)。 
（二）進口 
8 月進口 601.0 億美元，為歷年單月第 3 高，較上月增 3.4%，較上
年同月增 44.3%(按新臺幣計算增 56.2%)；累 計1 至 8 月進口 4,374.3
億美元，較上年同期增 40.4%(按新臺幣計算增 42.4%)。 
（三）出入超 
8 月出超 223.0 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 55.2 億美元；
累計 1 至 8 月出超 1,369.1 億美元，較上年同期增 500.5 億美元。

### Page 2

二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
受惠於人工智慧(AI)、 高效能運算及雲端服務等應用持續擴增，相關
產品出貨維持強勁， 加上價格攀升效 應，8月出口較上年同月增 239.7
億美元(+41.0%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 電子零組件出口 322.2 億美元(歷年單月新高)， 較上年同月增118.3
億美元(+58.0%)，其中積體電路增 115.5 億美元(+60.0%)、印刷電
路增 1.4 億美元(+29.9%)、 電容器及電阻器增0.7 億美元(+24.9%)。 
2. 資通與視聽產品出口 331.0 億美元，較上年同月增 97.6 億美元
(+41.8%)，其中電腦及其附屬單元增68.7 億美元(+40.4%)、儲存媒
體增 19.1 億美元(+2.4 倍)、 交換器及路由器增9.7 億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
3. 礦產品出口 20.0 億美元，較上年同月增 8.3 億美元(+70.2%)，其
中石油煉製品增 7.8 億美元(+74.3%)。 
4. 基本金屬及其製品出口 29.6 億美元，較上年同月增 5.4 億美元
(+22.4%)，其中銅及其製品增 6.1 億美元(+1.5 倍)，鋼鐵及其製品
減 1.1 億美元(-8.7%)。 
5. 機械出口 26.8 億美元，較上年同月增 3.6 億美元(+15.2%)，其中
生產半導體等機械增 1.9 億美元(+42.1%)、未列名具特殊功能機械
增 0.4 億美元(+39.0%)。

累計 1 至 8 月出口較上年同期增 1,759.8 億美元(+44.2%)，以資通與
視聽產品增 1,023.7 億美元(+68.5%)、電子零組件增 629.5 億美元
(+45.8%)增加較多。

生產等出現結構性轉變，爰另予併計。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
由於 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求之帶動，積體電路及資通產
品進口維持強勁，加上輸入物價明顯上漲，8 月總進口較上年同月增
184.5 億美元(+44.3%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口408.4 億美元， 較上年同月增129.4億美元(+46.4%)，
其中電子零組件增 82.5 億美元(+62.6%)、礦產品增 16.9 億美元
(+38.4%)、電腦之零附件增 14.6 億美元(+84.4%)、基本金屬增 4.5
億美元(+21.8%)、塑化原料增 4.0 億美元(+16.2%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 130.6 億美元， 較上年同月增 38.4 億美元(+41.6%)，
其中資通設備增 27.2 億美元(+1.2 倍)、半導體設備增 2.8 億美元
(+7.3%)、交通運輸設備增 2.8 億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
3. 消費品進口 55.7 億美元，較上年同月增 16.0 億美元(+40.5%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增12.1 億美元(+3.7 倍)、藥粧用品增 1.2
億美元(+17.4%)、手機增 1.1 億美元(+78.1%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
8 月對陸港、日本出口創新高，對美國、東協為單月第 3 高 
1. 對美國出口 236.7 億美元，較上年同月增 40.6 億美元(+20.7%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 23.0 億美元(+14.6%)、電子零組件增 11.6
億美元(+1.5 倍)、電機產品增 1.7 億美元(+30.1%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 211.5 億美元，較上年同月增 59.7 億美元
(+39.3%)，其中電子零組件增 47.4 億美元(+45.6%)、資通與視聽
產品增 6.7 億美元(+42.9%)、 基本金屬及其製品增4.6 億美元(+1.2
倍)。 
3. 對東協出口 151.3 億美元，較上年同月增 44.9 億美元(+42.2%)，
其中電子零組件增 25.3 億美元(+50.3%)、資通與視聽產品增 12.6
億美元(+41.1%)、礦產品增 4.6 億美元(+1.5 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 44.5 億美元，較上年同月增 11.5 億美元(+34.8%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 6.6 億美元(+57.0%)、電子零組件增 4.0 億美
元(+1.1 倍)，基本金屬及其製品減 1.4 億美元(-31.0%)。 
5. 對日本出口 36.6 億美元，較上年同月增8.9 億美元(+32.0%)，其中
電子零組件增 8.9 億美元(+76.7%)、 化學品增0.5 億美元(+32.3%)，
資通與視聽產品減 0.6 億美元(-13.9%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 8 月對美國出口 1,803.2 億美元，較上年同期增 631.6 億美
元(+53.9%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,394.7 億美元，增 307.5 億美
元(+28.3%)， 對東協出口1,150.7 億美元 ， 增372.3 億美元(+47.8%)，
對歐洲出口 376.6 億美元 ， 增130.7 億美元(+53.1%)， 對日本出口247.0
億美元，增 54.7 億美元(+28.4%)。

五、主要進口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
8 月自中國大陸與香港進口 126.8 億美元 ， 較上年同月增47.6 億美元
(+60.2%)，自東協進口 106.9 億美元，增46.7 億美元(+77.6%)，自南
韓進口 91.7 億美元，增29.6 億美元(+47.7%)，自日本進口 57.0 億美
元，增 11.2 億美元(+24.4%)，自美國進口 53.9 億美元，增 14.3 億美
元(+35.9%)，自歐洲進口 49.5 億美元，增 6.2 億美元(+14.3%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 8 月自主要國家/地區進口與上年同期比較 ， 自東協增75.4%、
自南韓增 63.2%、自中國大陸與香港增 40.5%、自美國增 24.4%、自
日本增 24.1%、自歐洲增 12.8%。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）今年 1-7 月主要國家/地區外貿景氣普遍上升，南韓(1-8 月)、香港、
新加坡出口動能尤為強勁，分別年增 52.8%、46.6%、38.3%；中國
大陸(1-8 月)、美國出口分別年增 19.3%及 14.6%，德國(1-6 月)、日
本各年增 10.9%、8.1%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步上揚，累計 1-7 月以香港進口年增 48.1%最
為顯著， 新加坡、 中國大陸(1-8 月)分別年增 37.0%、27.0%，南韓(1-8
月)、德國(1-6 月)各增 18.7%、11.7%，日本、美國各增 6.5%、1.2%。

圖 5 2026 年主要國家/地區出進口年增率
七、綜合分析
（一）得益於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及 雲端服務等應用 需求強勁， 加
以外銷產品價格攀升，8 月出口 824.0 億美元，創歷年單月新高，年
增 41.0%，連續 34 個月正成長；進口 601.0 億美元，年增 44.3%，
主因 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求擴張 ，及農工原料行情上
漲；出、進口互抵後，出超 223.0 億美元，亦創單月新高 。累計 1
至 8 月出口年增 44.2%、進口增 40.4%。
（二）8 月出口主要貨品以電子零組件、資通與視聽產品貢獻最多，年增率
分別為 58.0%及 41.8%，前者受惠 AI 商機及記憶體外銷價量齊揚，規
模值創單月新高，兩類併計增 49.3%，其餘貨類平均增 16.3%，以礦
產品隨油品售價上漲，年增 70.2%較為突出，基本金屬及其製品、電
機產品各增 22.4%、22.3%。累計 1 至 8 月資通與視聽產品出口值已
超越歷年水準，與電子零組件合計占總出口比重達 78.7%。

（三）8 月對主要市場出口同呈雙位數成長，其中對中國大陸與香港、日本
受電子零組件外銷強勁推升，出口值同創單月新高，各年增 39.3%、
32.0%，對東協、歐洲、美國分別增 42.2%、34.8%、20.7%；五大市
場之外，對墨西哥及澳大利亞出口皆增逾 2 倍。累計 1 至 8 月對美
國、歐洲出口各年增 53.9%、53.1%，對東協年增 47.8%，對日本、
陸港各增 28.4%、28.3%，對五大市場出口規模均為歷年同期最佳。
（四）展望未來，地緣政治衝突 及美國關稅政策變化等不確定 因素，持續
干擾全球經濟成長力道，惟隨人工智慧應用 不斷深化，算力需求攀
升，國際雲端服務業者加速建置 AI 基礎設施，加以國內半導體及伺
服器等供應鏈具競爭優勢，均有助支撐我國外銷動能，下半年出口
可望維持成長態勢。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 聯準會上周三決議升息一碼至 3.75%-4.00%區間，FOMC 政策聲明顯示，18 名
FOMC 成員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次 ， 另有4 人認為存在升息兩次的可
能性 ，加上聯準會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)會後表達控制通膨的偏鷹立場 ， 帶動美
元指數走升至 100 高位。
⚫ 歐元區 9 月 ZEW 經濟景氣指數自前月的 31.4 降至 25.8，低於預估的 39.9。數
據下滑顯示在美伊衝突未獲緩解 、 能源價格持續高位情況下 ， 市場對於歐元區經
濟前景抱持較不樂觀的態度。
⚫ 美國公布初請失業金人數為 19.6 萬人，優於市場預期的 20.8 萬，再度呼應聯準
會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)認為目前勞動力市場仍具韌性的說法， 在美國經濟穩
健及通膨頑固的情況下，預計聯準會將持續升息，有利美元持穩。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委暨葡萄牙央行總裁佩雷拉(Alvaro Santos Pereira)表示，歐元區目前

通膨雖未廣泛擴散 ， 惟考量近期天然氣價格飆升 ， 央行將密切關注未來通膨壓力
擴散情形。最新利率期貨市場顯示，歐洲央行今年底仍有再升息一碼空間。
⚫ 日本政府通過消費稅減稅及家庭現金補貼的政策大綱，預計自明年 4 月起的兩
年內，將食品消費稅率自 8%降至 1%，惟大綱中未明確說明資金來源，再度引
發市場對於日本政府的財政擔憂，短線不利日圓表現。
⚫ 台灣央行 9/17 宣布重貼現率連 10 凍維持在 2%不變，央行總裁楊金龍表示，台
灣的通膨相較其他主要經濟體溫和，採取漸進緊縮貨幣政策有助物價穩定。 此
外，目前通膨率為 2%，明年有望降至 2%以下，後續將觀察通膨發展、主要經
濟體貨幣政策緊縮程度及台灣金融情勢，以決定未來貨幣政策走向。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.650，低點在 31.958，以 31.808 作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 1 角 7 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶 ， 美國CPI 數據公布後加強市場對於聯準會 9 月升息的預期 ，
早盤台幣跳空開低，外資連續三日賣超台股並匯出，加以出口商態度轉為惜售，令
台幣接連貶破 31.700、31.800、31.900 關卡， 週四由於聯準會偏鷹派升息，美元指
數走升至 100.300，早盤台幣跳空開低，台幣最低貶至31.958，不過隨後出口商賣壓
出籠，外資雙向進出，台幣收復部分跌幅，維持在31.900 附近震盪；臨近周末，外
資買超台股並於外匯市場匯入 ， 台幣走升至31.722， 最終在央行調節之下收在31.808。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場而言 ， 美國最新初領失業救濟金人數較預
期減少一萬多人、續領失業救濟金人數亦下降，顯示美國勞動力市場尚屬穩健，加
以聯準會升息抗通膨，鷹派立場給予美元指數支撐、不利台幣表現；就台灣市場來
看，九月台灣央行並未升息，台美利差擴大預計將帶給台幣壓力，此外，儘管聯準
會升息利空出盡，外資買超台股帶動台幣走升，惟台灣即將迎來中秋連假，預計金
融市場交投謹慎，將令台幣陷入橫盤整理格局，綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢區間
震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Sep/21/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走升。人民幣中間價連續走升，接連升破 6.7700、6.7600 整數
關口，升至三年七個月來高點，帶動人民幣偏升，不過由於美元指數震盪走強，加
以市場靜待聯準會利率決議公布 ， 交投謹慎 ， 人民幣升幅有限 ， 大致維持高檔震盪 ，
週四聯準會鷹派升息一碼，惟人民幣中間價連續五日走升至 6.7521，並創下四年高
點，帶動人民幣緩步走升，USD/CNH 升破 6.7000 整數大關，終場境外人民幣收在
6.6944，週線升值 94 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6984，週線升值 21 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣震盪走升 。 就國際市場來看 ，FED 最新政策聲明和經濟預測顯
示，聯準會為明年繼續收緊貨幣政策敞開了大門，美元指數走升，抑制人民幣升值
的速度；就中國國內市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實
質拋匯需求將支撐人民幣表現； 最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中 ， 匯價維持
在五日均線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，加以USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.7000 整數關
卡，顯示 USD/CNH 空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪走升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6700-6.7200

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週日圓走貶。 週初，美國通膨數據維持高位 ，令聯準會升息預期升溫 ，USD/JPY
自 153.29 低點反彈至 155 附近；週間 ，美國聯準會宣布升息一碼 ， 帶動USD/JPY 上
漲至 156.41；臨近週末，日本央行如市場預期升息一碼，惟央行偏鴿派立場令日圓
續貶，帶動 USD/JPY 一度最高來到 158.05，而隨後在日本政府於外匯市場進行匯率
詢價下，USD/JPY 回吐部分漲幅，終場收在 156.86，週線上漲 349 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本政府上周通過消費稅減
稅及家庭現金補貼政策大綱，惟大綱中未明確說明財源，再度引發市場對於日本政
府財政惡化的擔憂，預計將不利日圓走勢。二、日本央行上周五決議將政策利率調
升一碼至 1.25%， 9 名決策委員中有 2 人對升息持反對意見，降低市場對日本央行
進一步升息的預期。另一方面 ， 聯準會上周三亦升息一碼至3.75%-4.00%區間 ，FOMC
政策聲明顯示，18 位官員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次，另有 4 人認為存在升
息兩次的可能性，聯準會升息立場明顯較日本央行更為鷹派，預計將支撐USD/JPY
走勢。三、日本財務大臣片山皋月近期再度重申，將於必要時採取外匯干預行動，
以維護外匯市場有序運行。四、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY 短中天期均
線向下發散走勢偏空，惟近期匯價在多次向下測試 154 均拉回，明顯下方有買盤支
撐。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：154.80-159.50 
前週交易區間

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，聯準會升息預期升溫加上歐元區 9 月經濟景氣指
數下滑，令歐元走跌至 1.1525；週間，美國聯準會鷹派升息立場帶動美元走升，歐
元最低來到 1.1460；臨近週末，歐元持續偏弱整理，終場收在 1.1483，週線下跌 107
點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、 歐洲央行管委卡薩克斯
(Martins Kazaks) 表示 ， 為避免美伊戰爭引發的燃料成本上漲開始傳導至工資和其他
商品價格，歐洲央行可能需要漸進式調升利率以抑制通膨。最新利率期貨市場顯示
歐洲央行今年仍有升息一碼的空間，預計將支撐歐元走勢。二、美國聯準會決議將
利率調升一碼至 3.75%-4.00%區間，聯準會主席華許會後偏鷹言論帶動美元指數走
升至 100 上方，預計將限縮歐元走升空間。三、歐元區 9 月 ZEW 經濟景氣指數自
前月的 31.4 降至 25.8，低於預估的 39.9。數據下滑顯示在美伊衝突未獲緩解、能源
價格持續高位情況下，市場對於歐元區經濟前景抱持較不樂觀的態度，將不利歐元
表現。四、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 近期回測年線未能有效突破後明顯
拉回轉為下行走勢，目前匯價承壓於各天期均線下方，空方動能較佔優勢。綜合以
上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1350-1.1590

                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.14-115.09.18 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.70% 
公債－10 年期-A15106 1.92 % ~ 1.921% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.86% ~ 1.861%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 本週央行理監事會議決議結果 ， 維持政策利率不變 ， 符合
市場預期 ， 且近兩週央行持續減發定存單 ， 令市場短錢充裕 ， 惟週五銀行間分
配略為不均，部分銀行有資金需求，因此短率持穩於近期區間。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.70%，利率高位較上週上升 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投清淡，5 年期指標券 A15108 週三等殖系統成交在 1.86%，10 年
期指標券 A15106 則未成交，最近成交利率為 1.92~1.921%。 
台灣央行理監事會議如市場預期維持政策利率不變 ， 總裁楊金龍表示 ， 今年國
內通膨仍屬可控，明年有望回落至 2%以下，市場對今年升息疑慮有所降溫，
對台債形成支撐 ， 惟國際債市走勢仍偏空 ， 預期台債殖利率短線仍呈高檔區間
盤整格局，後續關注下週三財政部公布的第四季公債發行計劃，預估 5 年券
區間為 1.79%~1.83%，10 年券區間為 1.90%~1.95%。

### Page 3

3
Cathay’s View市場策略思維
市場焦點
1. 市場焦點:
• 地緣政治風險:
1)槍口下的談判? 中東局勢詭譎: 伊朗向美方提出七大停戰條件, 但與此同時, 葉門「青年運動」仍大
規模襲擊沙國油槽。美國面臨期中選舉壓力, 短線局勢不致大幅升溫; 惟選後(11/3)不確定性仍高。
2)俄烏衝突升級為俄歐戰爭? 歐洲多國提高安全警戒, 以防範俄羅斯的「混合戰」攻勢。預計北約將
持續加大國防支出, 有利國防類股前景。
• 聯準會跟進升息後 , 市場緊縮預期維持高位 : 聯準會啟動升息後 , 目前市場定價未來 12個月仍有再升
3~4碼空間。預計今年Q4殖利率維持高檔, 升息預期導致殖利率曲線趨平。
• AI需求支撐下, 科技股呈現跌深反彈: 雖AI末世論一度導致科技股賣壓, 惟基本面仍反映相關需求強勁:
1)台灣8月外銷訂單突破千億美元、全年上看1.1兆美元, 2)美光將公布財報, 仍將反映記憶體短缺情形。
2. 後續看點:
• 美國PCE(9/30)、非農(10/2); 川習會(9/24); 美光財報(9/30)。策略思維

1) 股市−短線市場持續波動, 中長期獲利成長仍佳: 市場情緒多空交錯, 並同步面臨高利率導致的評價壓抑效果,
留意短期波動風險; 中長期AI投資循環支撐營收動能、獲利前景, 基本面仍支持中長期多頭趨勢。
2) 債市−升息預期帶動下, 利率維持高檔: 近期美、歐、日三大行同步升息, 市場未來均將持續升息, 將延長利
率higher for longer時長。債市布局逢高鎖利, 中短天期為主、降低槓桿率以減少利率敏感度。
3) 匯市−鷹派Fed帶動美元反彈, 後續維持區間盤整: 聯準會轉鷹, 帶動美元扭轉下半年以來的相對貶勢。惟主
要央行同步升息之下, 美元大幅升值的空間亦相對有限, 預計短線DXY指數於100關卡附近震盪。

Fed模型推估: 核心PCE仍維持僵固性 短期通膨預期較5月高點降溫, 惟中長期預期持續墊高
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/9/22。 資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/9/22。
• 美伊衝突導致的高油價影響, 仍為短期通膨的主要變數; 但隨著高油價的維持時間延長(9月西德州原油月增17%、
汽油月增6%、柴油月增15%), 民生消費壓力、公共運輸成本同步升高, 不排除將進一步擴散至商品、服務等更廣
泛領域的可能性, 推升第二輪通膨擔憂。
• 以克里夫蘭Fed nowcast模型觀察, 核心PCE自2026年初以來便持續上修, 近期預估核心PCE年增率將落在3.4%附
近。市場預期方面, 1Y1Y通膨預期雖較5月高點回落, 但未來5~10年的通膨預期仍持續墊高。
• 市場目前已大致定價 9月、12月各升息一次 。惟通膨走勢持續偏離下 , 仍需審慎看待Fed提前至10月升息的風險,
乃至於10、12月均上調利率的可能。

FOMC會議後, 多數官員持鷹派立場 Fed談話持續偏鷹, 隱含潛在升息空間
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9/22。
註: Fed NLP指數轉正代表政策態度轉趨鷹派, 歷史在轉正並持續數月上升的狀態下, 升息或
高利率延續的機率上升, 並領先政策利率調整約1~3季。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9/22。
• 9月升息後, 多數Fed官員表態支持進一步升息, 反映Fed態度通膨風險仍維持高度警覺。部分官員已聚焦在能源衝
擊的後續傳導效果、通膨走勢持續偏離Fed目標的擔憂。另方面, 本週公布標普製造業、服務業PMI分別創52個月、
59個月新高, 進一步帶動各天期美債殖利率同步攀升。
• 在經濟成長維持韌性、失業率仍處低檔下, 本輪Fed緊縮更接近「預防性升息」邏輯, 而非景氣過熱所引發的需求
型通膨。然而, 市場升息預期仍偏強勁, 預估今年Q4殖利率維持高檔震盪; 近期殖利率曲線趨平, 惟走勢偏向熊平。
• 債市策略: 布局仍以息收為主, 不宜過度期待資本利得, 並適度縮短天期以降低利率敏感度。

AI需求蓬勃發展, 輝達出貨量持續升溫 AI發展重心轉向推論, CPU需求將大幅增加
科技｜Muse需求爆紅, 反映AI的發展已從訓練轉向推論
• Meta推出的AI代理人Muse近期快速爆紅, 不僅登上美國App Store免費榜前列, 更重新點燃市場對AI需求持續升溫
的期待。與傳統大型模型以GPU訓練為主不同, AI Agent在實際執行任務時, 需要大量CPU負責推理、任務編排、
資料傳輸與背景運算, 因此AI需求的擴張已開始從GPU進一步延伸至CPU。市場也因此重新上修CPU需求預期, 從
2026年的320億美元, 上升到2030年的2800億美元。
• 從投資角度來看, Muse的意義不只是單一AI應用爆紅, 更重要的是驗證AI Agent可能成為下一階段AI普及的重要入
口。近期Intel、AMD與ARM股價同步大漲, 正反映市場開始重新評價CPU在AI時代的角色, AI硬體投資主線也因此
從GPU進一步擴大至CPU。
資料來源: MS, 截至2026/8。 資料來源: MS, 截至2026/9。

AI需求旺盛, 外銷訂單單月突破千億 外銷訂單領先出口
台灣｜AI需求暢旺, 有望驅動外銷訂單與出口訂單續創新高
• 台灣外銷訂單持續展現強勁動能, 8月外銷訂單達1,029.6億美元, 月增5.1%、年增71.4%, 首度突破單月千億美元,
創下歷史新高, 並連續19個月維持正成長。主要動能仍來自AI、高效能運算與雲端需求, 其中資訊通信產品及電子
產品訂單分別年增100.5%及83.9%, 顯示AI供應鏈需求仍在快速擴張。此外, 經濟部也預估9月外銷訂單可望續創
新高, 預估介於1060-1091億美元。
• 由於外銷訂單相較出口更具有前瞻性, 且領先約1-3個月, 隨著外銷訂單屢創新高, 預估台灣全年出口金額有望進一
步上修, 並突破9,000億美元, 也將驅動台灣GDP進一步上調。

• 台股近期重拾漲勢, 電子權值股領軍上攻, AI ASIC、先進封裝、PCB及高速傳輸等族群輪動走強, 加權指數再度創

支撐台股中長期行情的主要動能。
• 值得注意的是, 指數雖位居高檔, 但企業獲利上修速度更快, 使台股本益比反而降至近期低檔, 呈現「股價漲、估值
降」的良性結構。這也意味本波行情並非完全由評價面擴張推動 , 而有企業獲利成長支撐 。短線指數漲多後波動
可能放大, 但若AI需求與企業獲利趨勢延續, 拉回仍可留意布局機會。

• 俄烏戰爭持續超過4年, 歐洲各國雖未「正式參戰」, 但近期波蘭、法國、德國、芬蘭等國已集體拉響安全警報, 全
面進入「混合戰(Hybrid Attack)」的實質備戰狀態。西方情報顯示, 普丁打算發動30萬大軍的隱形動員, 將戰火向
外延伸。作為回應, 烏克蘭於9/19向俄羅斯發射了超過1000架無人機, 創下開戰以來最大規模無人機襲擊紀錄。

• 在戰爭風險加大, 美國也要求歐洲各國擴大國防預算的背景下, 歐洲國防股長期需求看好。雖然油價高漲推升歐洲
通膨, 推高企業經營成本, ECB升息, 也壓抑股價評價, 國防相關族群陷入盤整, 但在長期基本面走強下仍可分批布
局。

10年美債利率位階仍高, 對新台幣形成壓抑 美元反彈與風險偏好拉鋸, 然而新台幣波動相對穩定
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9/23。
註: 統計8/21為美元受debasement交易影響下的波段低點; 各幣別升貶為兌美元換算。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9/23。
• 下半年市場聚焦美元debasement、歐亞央行升息、日本官方干預, 推動整體亞幣走升 。但在8月下旬以來, 隨著
市場進一步定價Fed升息路徑, 支撐美元自8/21的低點有所反彈。
• 新台幣波動相對穩定: 隨台股創高(近期外資持續加碼)、油價與美債利率漲勢暫歇, 帶動新台幣回穩。
• 新台幣多空因素並存, 料短期維持區間震盪格局, 主因: 1)Fed偏鷹立場將支撐美債利率續居高位, 支撐美元資產吸
引力。然而, 2)台灣AI供應鏈帶動出口、貿易順差持續擴張, 8月外銷訂單1029.6億美元(年增71.4%)再創單月新高,
並留意臨近季底結匯需求, 為新台幣潛在催化劑。

美中雙方可能會晤的議題 美對中出口關稅稅率 vs 人民幣匯價走勢
中國｜川習二會針對貿易與AI; 人民幣延續偏強走勢, 避免貿易齟齬
• 為因應「川習二會」, 官方在7月政治局會議便定調「發力提效」, 並強調「及時出台增量政策」。不可否認, 若能
打破科技圍堵, 將是極大鼓舞; 然而在美中結構性對抗的大背景下, 雙方最可能的交集, 仍僅止於延長貿易休戰與建
立AI對話機制。因此, 這次會面較高機率維持「格局不變、突破有限」的結果。
• 後續, 10月召開的五中全會將主軸放在黨建與反腐, 以「持之以恆推進全面從嚴治黨」為最核心議程, 預期經濟領
域不會是本次會議焦點。這也解釋, 為何官方在五中全會前對於增量政策, 依然保持克制的態度。
• 誠然, 人民幣主要受美中利率政策等因素影響。但就貿易戰角度而言, 不難看出兩次貿易戰時, 人民幣匯價都較為

溫吞, 也顯示官方在關稅疑慮下, 常以貶值作為緩衝; 反之, 進入協議期, 一方面是不確定性降低、風險偏好轉佳, 再
者, 官方也偏好以升值來降低貿易對手的齟齬, 加上人行長線人民幣國際化政策下, 也有利匯價延續偏強震盪走勢。
資料來源: Wind, 國泰世華銀行整理。 資料來源: Wind, 截至2026/9/23。

官員談話 消費者信心與價格預期
• 近期官員發言整體偏鷹, 但市場對ECB 10月再升息的定價仍僅約40%, 之所以未將10月升息視為基本情境, 主要在
於目前能源衝擊尚未明顯傳導至服務業及更廣泛的核心通膨 。即使ECB首席Lane預期食品、電力及商品價格將面
臨上行壓力, 但他也坦承截至目前為止, 服務價格外溢效應仍相對有限。
• 此外, 最新歐元區9月消費者信心指數由8月的-15.5下降至-16.5, 不僅結束連續四個月改善趨勢, 也略差於市場預期
的-16.0, 反映能源價格上漲壓制家庭支出與經濟前景信心。綜合而言, ECB內部論調正逐漸朝「higher for longer」
靠攏, 但疲弱的消費信心與尚未全面擴散的核心通膨壓力, 使市場仍保留約60%的機率認為ECB將在10月按兵不動。

除非月底9月通膨數據出乎意料, 方能使10月升息機率進一步上修。

英國利率下移 CBI工業趨勢調查
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9/23。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/8。
• BOE內部因應近期通膨反彈而維持3.75%基準利率不變, 且票委投票仍維持 6 比 3 的鷹派防禦姿態, 但實質上QT
模式的微調已有效減輕了短期內英國政府的發債融資成本 。隨著主動縮表計劃至 2027年4月前暫停、部分債券改
由直接轉售給財政部(DMO), 英債殖利率預計將擺脫無序暴漲的極端狀態。
• 疊加剛公布的9月CBI工業價格預期指數從22驟降至12, 反映出實體經濟內部通膨螺旋的生產端報價意願明顯降溫 。
促使近一週英國國債殖利率曲線出現整體顯著下移, 修正了此前過度悲觀的「期限溢價」, 讓原本極其陡峭的曲線
長端結構性回落。展望未來, 政策執行的微調與經濟領先指標的傾斜, 為英國的利率政策供階段性不變的思維支撐,

此即10月會議可能按兵不動。

Document 149: 9月PMI数据解读：9月制造业PMI回升至荣枯线之上

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月PMI数据解读：9月制造业PMI回升至荣枯线之上.pdf
Report source: 国新证券股份
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

市场研究部 2026年 9月 30日
事件
9月 30日，国家统计局服务业调查中心公布，9月制造业采购
经理指数（PMI）为 50.1，非制造业商务活动指数为 50.2，综合
PMI 产出指数为 50.7。
解读
一、9 月制造业 PMI 小幅回升至荣枯线之上。
9月份，制造业采购经理指数（PMI）为 50.1%，比 8 月上升
0.3个百分点，时隔 2个月升至扩张区间。
从分类指数看，构成制造业 PMI 的 5 个分类指数中，生产指
数、新订单指数和供应商配送时间指数均高于临界点；原材料库存
指数、从业人员指数低于临界点。
生产指数为 51.7%，比 8月上升 1.3个百分点，表明制造业生
产活动扩张加快。
新订单指数为 50.5%，比 8月下降 0.1个百分点，高于临界点，
表明制造业市场需求继续改善。
原材料库存指数为 48.2%，比 8月上升 0.1个百分点，表明制
造业主要原材料库存量降幅略有收窄。
从业人员指数为 48.4%，比 8月下降 0.3个百分点，表明制造
业企业用工景气水平回落。

供应商配送时间指数为 50.1%，与 8月持平，高于临界点，表
明制造业原材料供应商交货时间继续加快。
9月主要原材料购进价格指数为 60.8，比 7月上升 4.2个百分
点，持续高于荣枯线，出厂价格指数为 54.0，比 7月回升 3.6个百
分点，上游原材料购进价格持续上涨，出厂价格继续上涨，企业效
益好转。
二、9 月非制造业 PMI 回升至荣枯线之上。
9月份，非制造业商务活动指数为 50.2%，比 8月上升 1.2个
百分点，时隔 2个月回升至荣枯线之上，非制造业景气水平明显回
升。
宏观研究
9月制造业 PMI回升至荣枯线之上
——9月 PMI数据解读
市场表现截至 2026.9.30
数据来源：国家统计局,国新证券整理。
分析师：郝大明
登记编码：S1490514010002
邮箱：haodaming@crsec.com.cn
证券研究报告

### Page 2

分行业看，建筑业商务活动指数为 50.3%，比 8月上升 3.4个
百分点；服务业商务活动指数为 50.2%，比 8月上升 0.9个百分点。
从服务业行业看，电信广播电视及卫星传输服务、货币金融服务、
保险等行业商务活动指数均位于 55.0%以上较高景气区间；资本市
场服务、房地产等行业商务活动指数均低于临界点。
新订单指数为 46.5%，比 8月上升 2.4个百分点，表明非制造
业市场需求景气水平回升。分行业看，建筑业新订单指数为 45.7%，
比 8月上升 3.3个百分点；服务业新订单指数为 46.7%，比 8月上
升 2.2个百分点。
投入品价格指数为 52.5%，比 8月上升 1.4个百分点，表明非
制造业企业用于经营活动的投入品价格总体水平继续上涨。分行业
看，建筑业投入品价格指数为 54.0%，比 8月上升 2.8个百分点；
服务业投入品价格指数为 52.2%，比 8月上升 1.1个百分点。
销售价格指数为 50.3%，比 8月上升 1.0个百分点，表明非制
造业企业销售价格总体水平有所回升。分行业看，建筑业销售价格

指数为 48.5%，比 8月下降 1.3个百分点；服务业销售价格指数为
50.6%，比 8月上升 1.4个百分点。
从业人员指数为 45.9%，比 8月上升 0.5个百分点，表明非制
造业企业用工景气度改善。分行业看，建筑业从业人员指数为
44.3%，比 8月上升 1.3个百分点；服务业从业人员指数为 46.2%，
比 8月上升 0.4个百分点。
业务活动预期指数为 55.5%，比 8月上升 0.5个百分点，表明
非制造业企业对市场发展信心有所增强。分行业看，建筑业业务活
动预期指数为 55.4%，比 8月上升 3.6个百分点；服务业业务活动
预期指数为 55.5%，与 8月持平。
三、9月综合 PMI 产出指数回升至荣枯线之上。
9月，综合 PMI 产出指数为 50.7%，比 8月上升 1.2个百分点，
升至临界点以上，我国企业生产经营活动总体较 8月扩张。
四、预测 10 制造业景气小幅上升。
9月制造业和非制造业业务活动预期指数分别为 53.8和 55.5，
企业对未来生产经营前景比较乐观，预测 10 月制造业景气小幅上

升，非制造业景气继续保持稳定。
投资建议
1：9月制造业和非制造业业务活动预期双双保持乐观。
2：电信广播电视及卫星传输服务、货币金融服务、保险等行
业商务活动指数均位于 55.0%以上较高景气区间。
3：9月制造业出厂价格指数继续上涨，制造业企业效益好转。

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng hóa Sài Gòn(SCS) 
Thách thức từ Long Thành 
22/09/2026  
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

KQKD 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ

Mạng bay Trung Đông phục hồi hỗ 
trợ sản lượng SCS từ 2H2026

Long Thành tạo áp lực lên sản lượng 
và biên lợi nhuận từ 2027, triển vọng 
dài hạn phụ thuộc khả năng tham 
gia vận hành nhà ga mới

Cổ tức 2026 dự kiến ở mức 5,000 
VND/cổ phiếu

Khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá mục 
tiêu 50,700 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, SCS ghi nhận doanh thu 279 tỷ VND (-4% yoy), LNST đạt 188 tỷ VND (đi 
ngang svck). Lũy kế 1H2026, doanh thu đạt 551 tỷ VND (-1% yoy), LNST đạt 361 tỷ 
VND (+0.5% yoy), trong đó thu nhập tài chính tăng mạnh bù đắp phần lớn sự suy 
giảm lợi nhuận từ hoạt động khai thác nhà ga.

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng mùa cao điểm hàng hóa cuối năm và mạng bay Trung Đông 
phục hồi sẽ hỗ trợ sản lượng SCS trong những tháng cuối năm. Tuy nhiên, mức độ 
cải thiện còn hạn chế do nhu cầu vận tải trên các tuyến Trung Đông – châu Á và 
châu Âu – Trung Đông vẫn suy yếu. Sản lượng hàng quốc tế/nội địa năm 2026 dự 
kiến đạt 209/52 nghìn tấn (-7%/-4% yoy).

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng sau khi các hạ tầng kết nối với sân bay Long Thành được hoàn 
thiện, tỷ lệ phân bổ chuyến bay quốc tế giữa Long Thành và Tân Sơn Nhất sẽ dao 
động quanh khoảng 75%/25%. Tỷ trọng phân bổ hàng quốc tế tại TP. Hồ Chí Minh 
qua Long Thành 2027/2028/2029 dự kiến sẽ đạt 10%/35%/55%.

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng SCS tiếp tục duy trì cổ tức tiền mặt cao nhờ khả năng tạo dòng 
tiền ổn định và quy mô tiền gửi lớn. Cổ tức năm 2026 dự kiến đạt 5,000 VND/cổ 
phiếu, trước khi duy trì ở mức 3,000 VND/cổ phiếu/năm từ 2027 nhằm tích lũy 
nguồn lực cho các dự án mới.

Dựa trên triển vọng kinh doanh và kết quả định giá, chúng tôi đưa ra khuyến nghị 
TRUNG LẬP với cổ phiếu SCS, mức giá mục tiêu 50,700 VND/cổ phiếu.

Cơ cấu doanh thu 2025 Hoạt động kinh doanh

CTCP Dịch vụ hàng hóa Sài Gòn được thành 
lập năm 2008, hiện là đơn vị khai thác nhà ga 
hàng hóa hàng không lớn thứ 2 tại cảng 
hàng không quốc tế (HKQT) Tân Sơn Nhất 
với thị phần khoảng 50%. Các cổ đông lớn 
của SCS bao gồm CTCP Gemadept (33.4%) 
và Tổng Công ty Cảng Hàng không Việt Nam 
(13.6%). 
Hoạt động khai thác nhà ga là mảng kinh 
doanh chính của SCS. Hiện SCS đang tiếp 
tục mua sắm trang thiết bị nâng công suất 
xử lý hàng lên 350,000 tấn/năm. 
Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Sản lượng hàng quốc tế/nội địa năm 2026 dự kiến đạt 209/52 nghìn tấn (-7%/-4% yoy) dù đã được hỗ 
trợ bởi mạng bay Trung Đông phục hồi và mùa cao điểm cuối năm 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Thông lượng hàng hóa quốc tế qua SCS bắt đầu giảm từ 2027 với 10%/35%/55%/70% hàng quốc tế 
Tân Sơn Nhất phân bổ sang cho L TA vào 2027/2028/2029/2030, trước khi bắt đầu tăng trưởng trở lại từ 
2031. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
SCS tiếp tục duy trì chính sách cổ tức tiền mặt ổn định nhờ nền tảng tài chính lành mạnh, cổ tức 2026 
dự kiến đạt 5,000 VND/cổ phiếu và duy trì ở mức 3,000 VND/cổ phiếu/năm từ 2027.

Biểu đồ 4. Cơ cấu thông lượng hàng SCS (tấn)  Biểu đồ 5. Giá dịch vụ và biên LNG hoạt động khai thác nhà ga 
của SCS

Nguồn: SCS  Nguồn: SCS

Mạng bay Trung Đông phục hồi hỗ 
trợ sản lượng SCS trong 2H2026, 
song mức độ cải thiện còn hạn chế 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng sản lượng hàng hóa của SCS sẽ cải thiện trong những tháng 
cuối năm 2026 nhờ mạng bay Trung Đông dần được khôi phục, đặc biệt từ 
Qatar Airways, cùng nhu cầu vận tải hàng hóa tăng trong mùa cao điểm cuối 
năm. Tuy nhiên, mức độ phục hồi dự kiến còn hạn chế khi nhu cầu vận tải trên 
các tuyến Trung Đông – Châu Á và Châu Âu – Trung Đông vẫn giảm lần lượt 
14.1% và 16.1% yoy trong tháng 7/2026 (tháng thứ 5 liên tiếp), trái ngược với 
mức tăng 3.9% của thị trường hàng hóa hàng không toàn cầu. Diễn biến này 
phần nào giải thích việc sản lượng hàng quốc tế của SCS tiếp tục giảm khoảng 
10% yoy trong tháng 7–8, dù tần suất bay của các hãng Trung Đông đã phục 
hồi đáng kể.

Chúng tôi dự phóng sản lượng hàng quốc tế và nội địa trong bốn tháng cuối 
năm lần lượt giảm 4% và 2% yoy, cả năm 2026 đạt 209.4 nghìn tấn (-7% yoy) và 
52.2 nghìn tấn (-4% yoy). Doanh thu khai thác nhà ga được kỳ vọng tiếp tục 
được hỗ trợ bởi việc điều chỉnh tăng giá dịch vụ từ đầu năm, bù đắp một phần 
tác động từ sản lượng suy giảm.

Biểu đồ 6. Mức độ phục hồi chuyến bay của các hãng hàng không vùng Vịnh 
Đến ngày 21/09/2026, tần suất bay của các 
hãng vùng Vịnh đã phục hồi đáng kể, phần 
lớn đạt khoảng 85–100% mức trước xung đột, 
riêng Qatar Airways đạt 97%, tăng từ mức 
87% ghi nhận giữa tháng 6.

### Page 5

Long Thành tạo áp lực lên sản 
lượng và biên lợi nhuận từ 2027, 
triển vọng dài hạn phụ thuộc khả 
năng tham gia vận hành nhà ga mới 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng nhu cầu vận tải hàng hóa hàng không khu vực phía Nam duy 
trì tăng trưởng trong trung hạn, với sản lượng hàng quốc tế/nội địa tăng 8%/4% 
yoy năm 2027 và 9%/6% mỗi năm từ 2028, được hỗ trợ bởi hoạt động xuất 
nhập khẩu, thương mại điện tử và sự phát triển của chuỗi cung ứng. Tuy nhiên, 
sản lượng hàng quốc tế qua SCS dự kiến chịu áp lực khi các chuyến bay từng 
bước chuyển sang Long Thành. Trong kịch bản cơ sở, chúng tôi giả định tỷ lệ 
chuyến bay quốc tế chuyển sang Long Thành lần lượt đạt 10%/35%/55%/70% 
trong giai đoạn 2027–2030, với tác động lên sản lượng thực tế phụ thuộc vào 
tiến độ khai thác nhà ga hàng hóa và hệ thống logistics tại sân bay mới.

Đồng thời, giá dịch vụ quốc tế dự kiến bắt đầu giảm từ giữa năm 2027 nhằm giữ 
chân các hãng hàng không, trong khi giá nội địa kỳ vọng duy trì tăng 3%/năm. 
Theo đó, biên lợi nhuận gộp dự kiến giảm nhẹ trong năm 2027 và chịu áp lực 
đáng kể từ 2028 khi tỷ trọng hàng quốc tế có mức giá dịch vụ cao suy giảm.

Bên cạnh đó, SCS có cơ hội mở rộng quy mô khai thác trong dài hạn thông qua 
việc tham gia vận hành nhà ga hàng hóa số 1 tại Long Thành, công suất thiết kế 
550 nghìn tấn/năm. Doanh nghiệp đã ký biên bản ghi nhớ với ACV về khả năng 
tham gia vận hành nhà ga này, qua đó có thể tiếp cận nguồn hàng dịch chuyển 
từ Tân Sơn Nhất nếu được lựa chọn. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi chưa đưa nguồn thu 
từ dự án vào kịch bản cơ sở khi chưa có kết quả lựa chọn đơn vị vận hành chính 
thức.

Biểu đồ 7. Thông lượng hàng và biên LNG của SCS 2022 – 2028F

Nguồn: SCS, KBSV dự phóng

Nền tảng tài chính lành mạnh và 
chính sách cổ tức tiền mặt ổn định 
hỗ trợ định giá cổ phiếu 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng SCS tiếp tục duy trì chính sách cổ tức tiền mặt ổn định trong 
những năm tới nhờ khả năng tạo dòng tiền cao, quy mô tiền gửi lớn và mức 
đòn bẩy tài chính thấp. Trong 1H2026, thu nhập tài chính tăng mạnh đã bù đắp 
phần lớn sự suy giảm lợi nhuận từ hoạt động khai thác nhà ga, giúp LNST duy 
trì ổn định ở mức 361 tỷ VND (+0.5% yoy). Chúng tôi dự phóng cổ tức tiền mặt 
năm 2026 tiếp tục đạt 5,000 VND/cổ phiếu, trước khi duy trì ở mức 3,000 
VND/cổ phiếu/năm từ 2027, phù hợp với triển vọng lợi nhuận chịu áp lực từ 
Long Thành và nhu cầu tích lũy nguồn lực tài chính để tham gia các dự án mới.

Khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá mục 
tiêu 50,700 VND/cổ phiếu  
 Sử dụng phương pháp chiết khấu dòng tiền (DCF), trên cơ sở cập nhật triển 
vọng kinh doanh và tác động từ quá trình chuyển đổi khai thác sang Long 
Thành, chúng tôi đưa ra giá mục tiêu cho SCS là 50,700 VND/cổ phiếu, tương 
đương với mức tiềm năng sinh lời 14.3% so với mức giá đóng cửa ngày 
22/09/2026. Trong kịch bản cơ sở, chúng tôi chưa đưa nguồn thu từ việc tham 
gia vận hành nhà ga hàng hóa số 1 tại Long Thành vào định giá. Các giả định 
chính bao gồm: 
- Thông lượng hàng quốc tế năm 2026 đạt 209.4 nghìn tấn (-7% yoy), với 
bốn tháng cuối năm giảm 4% yoy do mạng bay Trung Đông chưa phục 
hồi hoàn toàn. Nhu cầu hàng quốc tế khu vực phía nam dự kiến tăng 
8% năm 2027 và 9%/năm trong giai đoạn 2028–2030, kéo theo lượng 
hàng qua SCS trong 2027/2028/2029/2030 sẽ giảm lần lượt -2.8%/-
21%/-25%/-27% yoy, trước khi tăng trưởng trở lại từ 2031 sau khi đạt 
tỷ trọng phân bổ tối ưu giữa SGN và LTA. 
- Thông lượng hàng quốc nội năm 2026 đạt 52.2 nghìn tấn (-4% yoy), với 
bốn tháng cuối năm giảm 2% yoy do áp lực chi phí vận tải và điều 
chỉnh mạng bay. Sản lượng dự kiến phục hồi 4% năm 2027 và 6%/năm 
từ 2028 khi chi phí nhiên liệu hạ nhiệt và năng lực khai thác cải thiện. 
- Giá nội địa dự kiến tăng 3%/năm, trong khi giá quốc tế bắt đầu giảm 
từ giữa 2027 nhằm giữ chân các hãng hàng không. Biên lợi nhuận gộp 
dự kiến giảm nhẹ trong 2027 và chịu áp lực đáng kể từ 2028 khi tỷ 
trọng hàng quốc tế suy giảm và chi phí cố định không giảm tương ứng

Document 230: DeepSeek扩招！弹性计算团队大量HC，尤其需要资深工程师

Source PDF: all report pdf/DeepSeek扩招！弹性计算团队大量HC，尤其需要资深工程师.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-03
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

现在 DeepSeek 的⼀套 DSec 扩展分⽚，⼤约有 160 台服务器、 3 万个 CPU 核⼼和 250TB 内
存，每天要服务约 300 万个沙盒。⾼峰期，同时在线的沙盒超过 38 万个，每秒还能创建
5000 多个。 DeepSeek 已经在⽣产环境⾥部署了多个这样的分⽚，能够⽀持数百万个沙盒
同时运⾏。接下来，要把 Agent 运⾏环境的数量和种类扩充成百上千倍。这么⼀看，确实得
招⼈ ……
摘要：
弹性计算团队⼤量 HC 招⼈！尤其需要资深⼯程师。
三周前不是刚招过⼀轮吗？咋⼜缺⼈了。
这次没发岗位 JD ，直接甩了⼀篇 DeepSeek 技术分享：
《 DeepSeek 弹性计算（ DSec ）：⾯向⼤规模 Agent 训练的沙盒基础设施》
现在 DeepSeek 的⼀套 DSec 扩展分⽚，⼤约有 160 台服务器、 3 万个 CPU 核⼼和 250TB 内存，每
天要服务约 300 万个沙盒。
⾼峰期，同时在线的沙盒超过 38 万个，每秒还能创建 5000 多个。
DeepSeek 已经在⽣产环境⾥部署了多个这样的分⽚，能够⽀持数百万个沙盒同时运⾏。

接下来，要把 Agent 运⾏环境的数量和种类扩充成百上千倍。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

这么⼀看，确实得招⼈ ……
38 万个沙盒同时在线
DSec 是⽀撑 DeepSeek-V4 全部训练、评测和数据预处理流程的沙盒基础设施。
从 DeepSeek-V3.2 ⼀路到 V4.1 ， Agent 训练、评测和数据预处理产⽣的全部沙盒负载，都已经运
⾏在 DSec 上。
它⾯对的⼯作负载和普通云计算还有点不⼀样。
Agent 训练过程中，模型需要不断进⼊真实环境执⾏任务。
读代码、改⽂件、安装依赖、执⾏测试、运⾏服务，每⼀步都会改变当前环境；下⼀轮交互⼜
要接着前⾯的状态继续。
因此这些沙盒既要能够快速、⼤批量创建，⼜不能每执⾏⼀步就销毁重来。
DeepSeek 总结下来，这类负载有⼏个很明显的特点：
创建请求会突然集中涌⼊；
CPU ⼤部分时间处于空闲状态，但为了保存⽂件和进程，内存⼜得⼀直留着；
不同 Agent 任务需要的执⾏环境差异很⼤；
基础镜像复⽤率不⾼；
训练过程还可能因为 GPU 资源被抢占⽽中断。
DSec 现在做的⼤量⼯作，就是围绕这些特点，把 Agent 环境的供给成本往下压。

⾸先是环境本⾝。

### Page 3

DeepSeek 拿 2026 年某⼀周的⽣产数据统计了⼀下，仅 Container 后端就⽤到了 11266 个基础镜
像、 102171 个⼯作区，以及数百个⼯具包。
如果按照传统⽅法，每种组合都做成⼀份完整镜像，代码仓库或者⼯具包稍微更新⼀次，就可
能跟着重建⼀⼤批镜像。
所以 DSec 直接把环境拆成了三层。
操作系统和基础软件放进 base image ，任务代码和依赖放进 workspace ， DeepSeek Harness 这
类⼯具则单独做成 toolkit 。
三部分分别管理版本，真正创建沙盒的时候再组合起来。
这样⼯具更新，只需要重建⼯具所在的那⼀层，不必把整个环境从头再做⼀次。
但环境拆开之后，还有另⼀个问题：
这么多镜像，要不要提前全部拉到服务器上？
DeepSeek 看了⼀遍真实⽣产数据，发现根本没必要。
Agent 实际运⾏时，真正访问到的数据只占整个镜像的 4.2% 到 13.3% 。
也就是说，⼀个⼏⼗ GB 的环境提前完整下载下来，其中绝⼤多数内容可能从头到尾都不会被

Agent 碰到。
DSec 于是把镜像数据统⼀放到 DeepSeek ⾃⼰的 3FS 分布式⽂件系统⾥，本地主要保存镜像元
数据，真正需要某块数据时再按需读取。
DeepSeek 集中创建 8192 个 Container 做过⼀次测试。
如果先把完整镜像拉到本地，整个任务需要 60 多分钟；换成按需加载之后，时间缩短到了⼤约
35 分钟，速度提升约 1.71 倍，磁盘写⼊量同时减少约 57% 。

### Page 4

另⼀个⼯作区实验⾥，原本每创建⼀个沙盒，都需要单独解压⼀份 tar.gz ⽂件。
改成直接挂载 EROFS 层之后，整个任务从 79 分钟缩短到 45 分钟，磁盘写⼊总量只剩原来的⼤约
1/5.5 。
环境供给解决之后，接下来就是怎么把 CPU 和内存尽可能利⽤起来。
DeepSeek 统计发现， Agent 沙盒其实相当 “ 稀疏 ” 。
⼤约 90% 的沙盒，平均 CPU 使⽤量都不到申请资源的 5% 。
原因在于 Agent 完成⼀次操作后，通常需要等待模型⽣成下⼀步动作。
等待期间 CPU 基本闲置，但⽂件、进程等环境状态仍然需要保存，所以内存⼜不能直接释放。

### Page 5

这给 DSec 留下了很⼤的资源调度空间。
DeepSeek 现在⽣产环境⾥的资源超卖率已经超过 50 倍。
不过，单纯往⼀台机器⾥塞更多沙盒还不够。
MicroVM 之间⼤量相同的只读内容如果各存⼀份，内存很快就会成为瓶颈。
DSec 通过 virtio-pmem 和 DAX ，让同⼀台宿主机上的 MicroVM 共享宿主⻚缓存。单独启⽤这项机
制，实验中的宿主机峰值内存占⽤相⽐基线下降了 40.2% 。
另⼀边，借助 DAMON 和 balloon 空闲⻚报告回收暂时不⽤的内存，按时间累计的内存消耗还能
再降低 21.2% 。
CPU 也不能简单⼀视同仁。
有些 Agent 任务对响应时间⽐较敏感，有些则晚⼀点完成也没关系。
DSec 会优先保证前⼀类任务，再让时延要求较低的任务利⽤剩余 CPU 。
DeepSeek 的实验⾥，当同⼀台机器上的其他任务已经占掉节点 50% 的 CPU 容量时，经过调度优
化，时延敏感任务受到的延迟影响从 45.2% 降到了 17.3% 。

### Page 6

镜像按需加载、内存共享、 CPU 调度 ……DSec 前⾯这些优化，⽬的其实都很⼀致：
尽可能压低每个 Agent 环境占⽤的资源，把同⼀批机器撑出更⼤的并发规模。
但资源省下来之后， DeepSeek 接下来还准备把 Agent 能训练的环境也⼀起铺开。
⽤ Agent 给 Agent 造环境
不同 Agent 任务对运⾏环境的要求完全不同。
DSec 现在已经统⼀⽀持四种执⾏后端： FnCall 、 Container 、 MicroVM 和 Full VM 。
FnCall 适合在线评测等短任务；
Container 主要承担软件⼯程和⼯具调⽤；
MicroVM 提供更强的隔离能⼒；
到了 Full VM ，则可以提供完整操作系统，⽀持 GUI 、图形渲染甚⾄ Android 应⽤。
随着 Agent 能⼒继续往外扩，环境也会越来越复杂。
⼀个 Coding Agent 可能只需要代码仓库、 Shell 和测试⼯具，但如果以后要让 Agent 操作更多真
实软件、系统和服务，训练阶段就得先把这些环境构建出来。

### Page 7

⽽ DeepSeek 现在的做法已经有点 Agent 套 Agent 了：
直接⽤ Agent 构建运⾏ Agent 的环境。
传统⽅式下，可能需要⼀套平台负责 Agent 训练，再单独搭⼀套平台⽣产 Agent 所需的环境。
DeepSeek 发现，负责构建环境的 Agent 本⾝就已经运⾏在环境⾥⾯。
于是⼲脆把两件事都塞回 DSec 。
DSec 为此做了⼀个叫 pack_diff 的机制。
Agent 在沙盒⾥把环境配置好之后，可以直接⽣成增量快照，把当前状态保存下来。以后需要同
样的环境，就可以从这个快照重新恢复。
甚⾄ Agent 每执⾏⼀步，都可以把当前状态保存成⼀个新的可复⽤环境。
这还带来了⼀个挺重要的能⼒，轨迹分叉。
⽐如 Agent 执⾏到第 k 步，接下来有三种不同⽅案可以尝试。
系统可以先在第 k 步保存⼀次快照，然后从完全相同的状态恢复出多个沙盒，让不同分⽀分别继
续探索。
前⾯的环境和数据可以共享，只有后续发⽣的变化需要单独记录，不需要把前 k 步重新执⾏⼀

遍。
对于⼤规模强化学习来说，这相当于把同⼀条 Agent 轨迹上的环境状态也变成了可以复⽤的数
据。
与此同时， DSec 还在把 Agent 执⾏和 GPU 训练进⼀步拆开。
以前 Agent 执⾏循环和 GPU 训练任务放在同⼀个 Pod ⾥。
⼀旦 GPU 训练任务被抢占， Agent 执⾏循环也会跟着中断。
恢复之后，还得重新播放之前的命令记录，把训练进度和沙盒⾥的真实状态重新对⻬。
从 DeepSeek-V4.1 开始，这部分执⾏逻辑被迁进了 DSec 。
Agent 沙盒和负责推进交互的 worker container 都运⾏在可抢占 GPU 资源池之外。
这样即使 GPU 任务中断， Agent 执⾏到哪⼀步、环境变成了什么样，都还能继续保存。
⽽环境状态能够被独⽴保存之后， Agent 执⾏就不必再和 GPU 训练绑死。
GPU 重新回来之后，训练可以直接从中断的位置继续。

### Page 8

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
26 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
不过，当 Agent 真的可以在环境⾥⾃由操作以后，另⼀个问题也来了：
模型开始⾃⼰找环境漏洞了。
DeepSeek 说，他们已经在真实⽣产环境⾥观察到 Agent 尝试各种⾮常具体的操作：
读取环境⾥残留的答案、伪造 RPC 请求，甚⾄直接覆盖 /bin/bash 来注⼊命令。
甚⾄还有 Agent 尝试调⽤ XFS_IOC_SWAPEXT 绕过访问控制。
背后的逻辑很简单，强化学习给模型的是奖励，只要环境⾥存在⼀条⽐正常完成任务更容易拿
到奖励的路径，能⼒⾜够强的 Agent 就可能把它找出来。
但这会直接污染训练和评测结果。
所以 DSec 现在⼜得负责给 Agent 划安全边界。
⽂件和 Socket 访问通过 AppArmor 限制，即使 Agent 拿到了管理员权限，相应规则依然有效；⽹

络侧则利⽤ eBPF 给每个沙盒设置⽩名单，限定它能够连接的地址、端⼝和协议。
但 DeepSeek 也明确承认，这些办法还解决不了所有问题。
⽐如 Agent 如果进⼀步找到内核漏洞，⽬前依然缺少⼀套通⽤的防御机制。
他们对此的判断是：
随着模型能⼒继续提⾼，和 Agent 之间的攻防还会⼀直持续下去。
活⼉有亿点多，所以 DeepSeek 需要更多开发伙伴，团队需要继续 Scaling 。
我问你，招到⼈之后是不是能把 V4.1 Pro 也抬上来了 ……

Document 244: Fed builds credibility with hike; one more likely in Dec

Source PDF: all report pdf/Fed builds credibility with hike; one more likely in Dec.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Fed builds credibility with 
hike; one more likely in Dec 
Summary 
Event: The US Federal Reserve hiked its key interest rate by 
25bps to 3.75-4.0%, in a unanimous decision. This marks the 
first hike since 2023, reversing the easing cycle that had 
taken rates down from 4.25% in September 2025 to a trough 
of 3.75% by December 2025. The revised projections 
indicate the Fed expects to raise rates by an additional 25bps 
this year, hold rates in 2027, and cut 25bps in both 2028 and 
2029. It upgraded the long-run rate slightly to 3.2%.  
Market reaction: The US yield curve mildly flattened , while 
the S&P500 index fell 0.5%. The policy rate sensitive US 2 -
year government bond yield jumped 7 bps to 4. 74% as 
markets factored in  three more 25bps hikes by June 2027 . 
The US 10-year yield rose 2bps to 5.02%, its highest since 
2007, while the 30-year yield was little changed. The USD 
index (DXY) rose 0.6%, breaking past its 50- and 100-DMAs. 
Investment strategy: Today’s move realises what we had 
been flagging in recent weeks i.e. the rising risk of a Fed rate 
hike following Fed Chair Warsh’s Jackson Hole speech and 
recent economic data prints, namely: a pick-up in job creation 
and inflation over the summer and a sharp rebound in oil 
prices due to the re-escalation of the Middle East conflict.  
This hike helps the Fed rebuild credibility as an inflation 
fighter. Given the unanimous decision to hike rates this week, 
we believe there will likely be one more hike before the end 
of this year, and one more in H1 2027 as we expect robust 
growth amid strong AI investments and a tight labour market, 
keeping inflation elevated.  That scenario would still be less 
than the three more rate hikes money markets are pricing 
after the latest Fed move. We believe policy rates can return 
to the 4.0 –4.25% range by end-2027, with the possibility of 
cuts in H2 2027  as the impact of tariffs and high oil prices 
start to fade towards the second half of 2027.   
Today’s refreshed Fed policy rate path is mildly bullish for the 
USD index; so we revise up our 3 -month target to 100. We 
also revise up our 3-month 10-year yield target to 5.0-5.25%.  
 The USD rose, while stocks fell after the Fed hiked rates 
and signalled one more rate hike this year; ultra-long 
bonds rose slightly as the Fed rebuild its inflation-
fighting credentials 
Asset performance: 15-Sep close to 16-Sep close

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

2026-27 and inflation projection for 2026; however, it 
still sees inflation cooling over the next two years

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 17 September 2026

Backdrop to the rate hike 
In hiking rates, Fed Chair Warsh reaffirmed the inflation 
threat to the US economy. US headline inflation accelerated 
to 0.4% m/m in August and stayed elevated on an annualised 
basis at 3.4% y/y. Energy prices , driven by the Middle East 
conflict, were a primary contributor. Oil prices have extended 
gains since the August inflation report, surging past USD 
100/bbl. Meanwhile, core inflation rose 0.3% m/m in August, 
beating estimates for a 0.2% rise . Also, US producer prices 
jumped 0.4% m/m in August, the most since May, and 5.4%  
y/y, further reinforcing the inflationary backdrop. 
The inflation report follow ed a stronger -than-expected jobs 
report for August. US monthly job creation has surged to an 
average 80,000 this year, from 10,000, tightening a job 
market that already faces a supply crunch from the Trump 
administration’s clampdown against immigration.  The 
combination of an acceleration in job creation and inflation 
over the summer, combined with a rebound in oil prices, 
supported our expectation for a Fed rate hike this week.  
Removing “a dose of accommodation” 
Warsh framed the decision to hike as removing "a dose of 
accommodation", rather than the start of an aggressive rate 
hiking cycle, and we agree with that characterisation. The aim 
is to bring financial and credit conditions more in line with the 
Fed's price stability objectives. To quote Warsh: “Our 
predominant focus is on the price stability side of our 
mandate. The plain fact is that inflation is too high, and has 
been for too long….This  summer’s inflation readings do not 
tell me that underlying trends have meaningfully improved.” 
He added that many categories in the last consumer and 
producer inflation reports are still showing price increases 
above 3% on a six- and 12-month basis.  
The unanimous vote to hike rates, contrasts with a 9 -3 split 
vote in July to hold rates. When combined with the pencilling 
in of one more hike by December, this suggests a majority of 
Fed policymakers have turned more hawkish over the 
summer. In doing so, they have helped rebuild the Fed’s 
inflation-fighting credentials and its independence against 
President Trump’s pressures to cut rates. This explains why 
long-end bond yields were stable after this week’s hike. 
Nevertheless, Warsh provided little guidance on the future 
rate path, as he held what was reportedly the shortest post -
meeting press conference on record for a Fed chair . This is 
consistent with his stated preference for less central bank 
communication. We expect more clarity once the Fed task 
forces submit their reports by year end. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist 
 US job creation has accelerated this year after a slump 
last year, tightening a job market already facing a 
potential crunch from an immigration clampdown

Are investors rational?
The stock market strength and resilience over the last 3 months appears to be a rational response to strong
earnings and only limited signs that higher energy prices are materially affecting broad corporate profits.
Our dbLumina gauge indicates investors have been irrational during market

drops and periods of geopolitical tensions What does it mean to be ‘rational’?
Risk-off periods:
The tariff fears in 2025 and then the Iran
war in 2026 were key buying opportunities
in equity markets. Yet, the overwhelming
fear exhibited by investors in these periods
largely meant they sold off risk assets in an
emotional and irrational response.
Risk-on periods:
It can be rational to buy into a rising
market. Our indicator suggests this is what
happened in Q3 as earnings growth

continued to be strong.
Fear Indicators:
Sharp, disproportionate reactions to
threats, widespread sell-offs on
announcements, panic, flight to safety
beyond what might be fundamentally
warranted.

### Page 9

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 1
A reallocation of global sovereign reserve assets is accelerating, spurred by
geopolitical uncertainty and the fear of trade and financial sanctions. Notably,
central banks are repatriating their physical assets, such as the Dutch and French

central banks which have recently moved gold reserves out of New York.
Furthermore, a World Gold Council survey reveals that 45% of global central banks
plan to increase their gold reserves over the next 12 months.
What the market underestimates: The market views the recent surges in gold prices
as temporary, cyclical safe-haven flows. In reality, sovereign institutions are
structurally preparing for a more fragmented global trade system where tariff

policies and sanctions may make dollar-clearing assets more difficult.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Interest rates: At a time when the "world is awash in debt", long-term interest rates
of some countries may be forced structurally higher to attract alternative private
capital.
Inflation: Unexpected dollar depreciation may increase the cost of imported goods
Equity Prices: High-growth US equity valuations, which are highly sensitive to the

long-term risk-free rate, may face multiple compression if bond yields are pushed
higher.
The market currently prices in a material, long-term GDP boost from AI-driven
productivity gains. However, some research from the IMF suggests that over-reliance
on standardised LLMs can produce a high degree of "sameness" and uniformity in
cognitive output. Furthermore, escalating intellectual property and copyright
litigation threaten to bifurcate the quality of AI models based strictly on developers'

licensing budgets.
What the market underestimates: Some investors assume that the exponential
curve of AI hardware capabilities translates directly into exponential economic
output. But there is a risk that if AI tools merely standardise corporate processes
rather than innovate them, and legal barriers restrict access to premium data, the
productivity boom may be less aggressive than expected.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
GDP growth: Structural growth ceiling if AI fails to deliver the multi-percentage

point boost to global productivity required to offset aging demographics.
Equity prices: A downward revaluation of the technology and software sector if
massive AI infrastructure expenditures fail to generate revenue according to existing
expectations.
Geopolitical fragmentation of global reserve assets The AI “sameness“ trap and the productivity stagnation1 2
8

### Page 10

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 2
Advanced economies are facing a dual demographic crisis: rapidly aging populations
and underfunded pension systems. This is forcing some older people to remain in or
re-enter the workforce out of financial necessity rather than choice.

What the market underestimates: Lower unemployment rates are celebrated as a
sign of economic strength. However, in structurally ageing societies like Japan and
parts of Europe, the growth of the labour force is increasingly driven by part-time,
older workers. Furthermore, G20 nations lack adequate, cohesive policy frameworks
to address these long-term demographic fiscal drains.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: An older workforce working out of necessity has less discretionary

spending power, potentially locking economies into persistent, structural demand-
side deflationary forces, offset by supply-side wage pressures in highly skilled
sectors.
GDP growth: Labour productivity growth that is lower than it might otherwise be as
the median age of the workforce climbs, limiting long-term potential GDP.
The geopolitical battleground is focussed on energy blockades (such as the Strait of
Hormuz) but underestimates the weaponisation of critical minerals. China currently

holds ‘escalation dominance’ over rare-earth mineral supply chains. The emerging
risk is the proactive restriction of these minerals to Western firms, such as recent
bans on supplying rare-earth inputs to some US firms.
What the market underestimates: The market treats rare-earth supply constraints as
temporary trade spat negotiation tactics. In reality, as Western nations aggressively
push for reshoring and friend-shoring, China is increasingly using its processing

monopoly as a long-term strategic blockade to try to slow Western high-tech,
defence and the energy transition.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: Upwards pressure on inflation as the cost of manufacturing electric
vehicles, semiconductors, and advanced electronics surges due to supply blockades
and the high capital cost of rebuilding localised processing facilities.
Interest rates: Central banks may be forced to keep policy rates higher to combat

the persistent supply-side inflation generated by supply-chain fragmentation.
The fragmentation of the EU’s political centre Critical mineral isolationism and ‘green’ supply chain
blockades3 4

### Page 11

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Emerging themes – part 1
Our dbLumina AI model helps us identify the key emerging themes that have the potential to
most impact markets and economies in the short term
While the previous focus was on the AI hardware squeeze and
commercial competition, the narrative is shifting towards AI's role as a

critical component of national security and geopolitical leverage.
— AI access: We see this in President Trump's framing of AI development as a
geopolitical race ("whoever wins AI wins").
— AI as a bargaining chip: Several examples over the last 12 months including the
2025 deal whereby AMD agreed to pay 15% of sales from certain AI chips to the
US government in exchange for an export license. Even if these deals change, the
trajectory is one where access to AI technology is tied to national economic and

security interests.
— Direct military & security applications: Several examples including, cyberattacks
on critical infrastructure (such as a UK power plant), the use of AI in developing
"exceptional" phages to fight E.coli, and the growing field of AI security
discussions.
Outlook: Expect more government intervention, the use of AI technology access as a
tool in diplomatic negotiations, and a greater focus on the dual-use nature of AI in
both commercial and military contexts. This will likely create regulatory and

operational uncertainty for tech firms.
The trend of reshoring is evolving into a more proactive diplomatic
strategy where nations are forming new, often transactional, alliances
to navigate a world less dominated by a single superpower.
— Proactive alliance building: Canadian Prime Minister Carney has been front and
centre making his first international trip to France and the UK in an effort to
diversify away from traditional reliance on the US. That follows other key

meetings between Japan and India, and more.
— Bypassing traditional powers: Greenland signalled its willingness to turn to
China for investment if Western countries do not provide support, reinforcing the
leverage of some smaller nations.
— Shifting security arrangements: The US pushed for a ‘reassurance force’ in
Ukraine, while French President Macron hosted a "coalition of the willing" to
discuss Europe's defence without US guarantees. Further examples include the

discussion of a Franco-German "nuclear sharing" arrangement.
Outlook: The assumptions of a bipolar world order is giving way to a more complex,
multi-nodal system. This will create greater emphasis on regional blocs and issue-
specific coalitions. The stability of traditional alliances like NATO and the G7 remain
deeply uncertain.
AI’s impact on geopolitics and national security The fracturing of multilateralism and the rise of "Middle
Power Countries" 1 2

### Page 12

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Emerging themes – part 2
These four key themes dbLumina identifies are sometimes ‘known’ by investors, however, they may be underestimated by the market,
particularly when there are some potentially series longer-term impacts
Previous talk of “sovereign fiscal fragility” has now manifested itself

into a severe market reaction. Although fears around government debt
and deficits pop up from time to time, the magnitude of September’s
bond sell-off has been compounded by oil price concerns
— Accelerating sovereign sell-off: Concerns about inflation and debt sustainability
are not new but the peaks in bond yields are. Yields on 10-year Treasuries, bunds,
and gilts all rose sharply, with bunds hitting their highest level since 2009.
— Fiscal policy as a direct market catalyst: The market is increasingly sensitive to

fiscal policy announcements. For example, in the UK postponed tax rises, led to a
spike in gilt yields. Similarly, France's political turmoil and concerns over its
budget deficit caused the Franco-German yield spread to widen significantly.
— The “awash in debt" narrative: Treasury Secretary Bessent's comment at the G20
that "The world is awash in debt" signalled that the issue is increasingly top-of-
mind for policymakers.
Outlook: Markets will be increasingly quick to punish any perceived fiscal

irresponsibility. Expect higher borrowing costs, currency volatility, and increased risk
of financial instability, especially for countries with high debt levels.
The strain in the US consumer and labour markets is now diverging
sharply from the health of large corporations, reinforcing the K-shaped
economy.
— Strong corporate performance: Earnings season for Q2 showed remarkably solid
global growth, and the resilience of large corporations was a recurring theme

that helped support equity markets despite other headwinds.
— Weak consumer & labour signals: Economic reports have showed some signs of
a weakening labour market, falling consumer confidence, and stress among low-
income Americans. The New York Fed's survey also showed households expect a
worse financial situation.
— Policy and market disconnect: While strong corporate performance depends on
a robust economy, the underlying consumer weakness is concerning. Expect the

market to continue its twitchy reaction to data, as investors struggle to reconcile
these conflicting signals.
Outlook: This theme is potentially the most consequential for the domestic US
economy. If corporate profits and equity markets "detach" from the real economy as
consumers perceive it, it could lead to significant social and political instability,
making economic policy even more difficult to navigate.
The return of global bond vigilantes The bifurcation of economies: corporate resilience vs.

consumer strain3 4

Document 293: Next Generation EU_ The programme is coming to an end, but its effects on economic activity will endure (2026-09-23)

23 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
NEXT GENERATION EU: THE PROGRAMME IS COMING TO AN END, BUT ITS EFFECTS ON ECONOMIC ACTIVITY WILL
ENDURE
Guillaume Derrien
A pillar of the NextGenerationEU programme, the Recovery and Resilience Facility (RRF) will expire on December 31, 2026. As of the
end of August 2026, slightly more than three-quarters of the total budget had been disbursed (EUR 440 billion out of EUR 573 billion).
Disbursing the remaining quarter (EUR 133 billion) by the end of the year will require a significant increase compared with the histo-

rical pace (EUR 90 billion per year). The goal of channeling these funds toward productive investment appears to have been achieved.
Tangible results for investment in Europe
According to Eurostat, public spending co-financed by the RRF totaled EUR 310 billion between 2020 and 2025. Nearly three-quarters (74%)
consisted of capital expenditures, including EUR 106 billion in direct public investment and EUR 122 billion in capital transfers to the private

sector (such as grants). This spending supported investment by businesses and households.
The list of major programmes funded by the RFF, published by the European Commission 1, shows that, within this scope, nearly two-thirds of
the funding went to transportation (primarily rail), energy infrastructure, and digital infrastructure (see Chart 1).
Another notable point: capital expenditures co-financed by the RRF increased significantly, reaching 0.4% of GDP for the European Union as a

whole in 2025 (see Chart 2). There are significant disparities among Member States, primarily due to differences in the amounts allocated to
them. Croatia (1.9%), Greece (1.2%), Italy (1%), and Spain (0.7%) received significant support, in contrast to France and Germany (0.1%). These
figures are consistent with the IMF’s estimate that the RRF added approximately 0.3 percentage points (pp) to EU growth in 2025 (more than
1 pp in Greece and Croatia, around 0.8 pp in Italy, and 0.5 pp in Spain) 2. Furthermore, as the first major joint European borrowing initiative, the

RRF made it possible to finance investment without increasing national budget deficits, giving several countries (Spain, Italy, Greece, Portugal)
valuable breathing room to consolidate their public finances.
1 Recovery and Resilience Scoreboard
2 The EU’s Recovery and Resilience Facility: Experience and Lessons for the Next Multiannual Financial Framework, WP/26/114, June 2026
SOURCE: EUROPEAN COMMISSION, Recovery and Resilience Scorecard

What impact on EU’s economic activity can we expect once this programme ends?
At first glance, effects will be limited. The end of the programme is expected to weigh on both public and private investment. However, the
strengthening of economic activity that we anticipate (see details in our latest Ecoperspectives – Q3 2026) rests primarily on German public

investment programmes and structural drivers (investment in AI, defence spending).
The ECB makes the same point in its September macroeconomic projections 3. It emphasises that the end of the RRF is expected to weigh
on public investment in 2027, while it anticipates an acceleration in business investment. Furthermore, RRF funds are being absorbed on
the ground only gradually. Since most disbursements are concentrated toward the end of the programme, the effects of the RRF will extend

beyond 2026.

Document 310: Rates Spark_ Widening fiscal woes

Report source: ING Think

### Page 1

The higher rates environment is testing risk sentiment as ﬁscal
concerns move to the forefront. Debt dynamics are no longer
deemed only a French problem; other European countries are
coming into focus. The ECB could soften its tone given
tightening ﬁnancial conditions, but eurozone CPI and US jobs
data are unlikely to give central banks much room

Michiel Tukker and Benjamin Schroeder
European bond markets are seeing broader spread widening after the
presentation of the French budget
Sentiment turning sour on European debt
problems
European bond markets are showing the ﬁrst signs of broader
contagion after the French budget presentation. We had speculated
it could provide temporary relief, as it marked an eﬀort toward
consolidation, but looking further ahead, spreads were more likely
to test around 150bp. Relief was even more short-lived than we
thought, and a renewed widening has taken the 10y OAT/Bund
spread to almost 140bp already.

### Page 2

Rates Daily
More worryingly, spreads of other highly indebted countries –
foremost Italy and Greece – have widened even more than French
spreads, with an almost 15bp widening over German Bunds in the
10y and even more pronounced widening at the short end. This
broader risk-oﬀ and ﬂight to safety and liquidity has accelerated
the Bund outperformance versus swaps, in the end showing that
Bunds have not lost their safe-haven status as some had feared,

given the muted reaction to geopolitical turmoil over the past
month.
The broader risk-oﬀ is also helping to curb the aggressive European
Central Bank rate hike discount. Market expectations have eased
from as many as four hikes over the next 12 months to at most
three hikes with around 70bp of tightening discounted through next
September. A tightening of ﬁnancial conditions through wider
spreads also reduces the need for policy rate hikes. This would be in

line with ECB President Christine Lagarde's argument that elevated
longer-dated yields already do some of the monetary tightening,
which she acknowledged at the recent EU parliamentary hearing.
One thing to keep in mind: a broader risk-oﬀ makes an intervention
by the ECB in bond markets more likely at the margin, as it is no
longer a French problem.
Friday's events and market view
An important day for data, which includes both eurozone
inﬂation numbers and the all-important (yet noisy) US

payrolls ﬁgures. The consensus sees eurozone core inﬂation
rising from 2.4% to 2.5%, which can still be considered
benign given elevated energy prices. The expectation for non-
farm payrolls is a decline from a high 162k in August to a
more moderate 85k for September. Such a number would
point to a still resilient jobs market.

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

India: RBI monetary policy committee 
meeting (7-Oct)

The macro environment has undergone 
significant changes since the RBI 
monetary policy committee’s (MPC) 
August policy meeting, with global oil 
prices rising past $100bl, and US Fed 
kickstarting their rate hike cycle. 
Domestically, core and headline 
continued to rise, laying the ground for 
inflation to quicken beyond 5.8 -6.0% in 
the second half as price risks broaden. 
Strong 1QFY27 GDP growth meanwhile 
pointed to the economy’s relative 
resilience to external shocks.

Besides fuel feeding into food costs, 
weather has also proved to be disruptive, 
with indications that the El Niño 
phenomenon could extend into 2027. 
International food benchmarks, including 
the UN Food Price index and the 
Bloomberg spot agricultural indice s, 
continue to climb. India’s food inflation 
has become more broad -based, shifting 
beyond vegetables and pulses to sugar, 
milk, protein groups, and edible oils. Sub-
par weather has contributed to this 
upmove, besides idiosyncratic factors 
like swing in production trends (cobweb), 
diversion for fuel usage etc.

After two years of normal monsoon, 
cumulative southwest rainfall this year is 
set to end with rainfall around 12 -13% 
below the long -term average, the most 
since 2015 and resulting in a slower 
build-up in reservoir levels.  
Global benchmark oil price gauge has 
averaged ~$93pb in FY27 year -to-date, 
up 36% yoy  vs FY26. Retail pump fuel 
prices were adjusted in quick succession 
in May26 and have been left unchanged 
since then, while other fuel products 
including CNG, aviation fuel, LPG etc 
have continued to climb. Add to this, 
other pockets like telecom, restaura nts, 
business services, transport fares etc 
have been inching up, reflecting early 
signs of broadening in price pressures (on 
sequential basis). A gradual broadening 
of price pressures is likely to keep 
headline inflation high and narrow the 
real rate buffer significantly.

Authorities are also conducting liquidity 
absorption measures through market 
operations like VRRR, open market 
operations and sell-buy FX swaps. This is 
likely to align the overnight call money 
rates to the benchmark repo rate, after 
having fallen sharply d ue to the surplus 
in the banking system.  Liquidity and core 
inflation have historically displayed a 
modest positive relationship, as per our 
study. As such, the RBI's inflation 
mandate has likely reinforced the push to 
drain excess liquidity.

We expect the RBI MPC to raise the repo 
rate by 25bp to 5.5% , citing pipeline 
inflationary pressures. Stance could be 
changed to withdrawal of 
accommodation to signal hawkish intent. 
We expect one more hike within 4Q26, 
though the odds of additional hikes have 
risen as oil prices stay stubbornly high 
and weather remains unfavourable.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 2, 2026

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Malaysia

We expect industrial production to grow 
by 5.8% yoy in August from 4.7% yoy in 
July. Strength in  export-oriented 
manufacturing is likely to persist , 
supported by sustained demand for 
artificial intelligence-related electronics 
goods, while mining output may have 
recovered from its contraction in July.

Taiwan

September trade and inflation data are 
forthcoming. Export growth is expected 
to sustain the 40% pace seen in the 
previous month. The global AI boom is 
facing rising headwinds from 
synchronised rate hikes by G3 central 
banks, higher bond yields, and rising  
financing costs. That said, there are so 
far no clear signs of a significant 
slowdown, based on the high -frequency 
indicators we track across AI demand, 
investment, and supply.

CPI is expected to rebound to 2.4% YoY 
in September, up from 2.0% in August. 
The earlier arrival of the Mid -Autumn 
Festival this year may have boosted food 
and services prices on a YoY basis. The 
rebound in global energy prices should 
also have lifted tran sport costs, 
including gasoline prices and airfares. 
While electricity tariffs will remain 
frozen, the December review implies a 
possibility of an electricity price hike 
after the local elections.

Given still -strong economic growth, 
persistent inflation, and the potential for 
inflation expectations to rise, we 
maintain our view that the central bank 
will raise the policy rate by a modest 
12.5bps to 2.125% in December.

Thailand

We expect Thailand’s headline inflation 
to accelerate to 3.2% yoy in September 
from 2.5% yoy  in August, slightly 
exceeding the central bank’s 1 -3% 
inflation target. Faster increases in 
energy and food prices likely pushed 
overall inflation higher, despite an 
easing in electricity tariffs starting 
September. We expect the Bank of 
Thailand (BOT), w hich views the rise in 
inflation as largely supply -driven, to 
keep its policy rate stable at 1.00% for 
the remainder of 2026, aiming to support 
an economy with low and uneven growth.

Probably 20% of the total inﬂuence. And of that, 70% reﬂects

Padhraic Garvey, CFA, Jan Frederik Slijkerman and Timothy Rahill
AI issuance pressure is having an impact on long-end rates, but so are inﬂation
and ﬁscal deﬁcits
The spending piece has a small, but not an
inconsequential impact
There are three basic AI components to account for, namely:
1. The spending piece,
2. The capital markets piece, and
3. The productivity piece.
The spending piece is relatively straightforward, as it's a straight
accounting in GDP. My colleague James Knightley has dealt with

### Page 2

this in detail here. Basically, AI accounts for a third of current
economic growth. The link here with rates is the tendency for higher
growth to be loosely associated with higher rates. However, given
that the economy outside of AI is less dynamic, the AI piece is more
of a ﬁller than anything else.
The capital markets issuance piece has a
more pursuasive impact
The capital markets piece is more complex. ING's Jan Frederik
Slijkerman did a deep dive into this space recently; it's here. For

2026, he identiﬁes TMT as a meaningful driver of additional
corporate issuance in the US, and something similar in Europe. Jan
Frederik goes on to argue that, while technology companies fund
the bulk of their investments from operating cash ﬂows, their
rapidly increasing capital spending is weighing on corporate
savings rates. As annual investment moves towards US$1 trillion,
and a chunk of this is reﬁnancing, the prognosis ahead is for
issuance volumes to remain elevated. So what you see in 2026

should at least be repeated in 2027 and beyond.
My colleagues Timothy Rahill and Marine Leleux have crunched the
latest corporate supply numbers for the US and Europe. They note
that year-to-date USD corporate issuance has hit US$878bn,
exceeding full-year corporate issuance recorded in every year from
2021 through to 2024, and running 54% above the same period
last year. The increase is driven by TMT, tripling to US$330bn. They
also note that issuers have favoured the long end of the curve. Year-

to-date issuance in maturities of 17 years and longer totals
US$235bn, making it the largest maturity bucket, ahead of 9–12
year tenors (US$219bn).
Equivalent issuance along the 10yr to 30yr part of the US Treasury
curve sums to US$340bn. That’s unchanged versus the previous
year. If we factor in buybacks, they are running at some US$20bn
above last year. So the swing factor from a US Treasuries
perspective is an eﬀective reduction in net issuance of the

aforementioned US$20bn. Contrast that with long-dated

Adding the two together, we have an overall 'delta' of some
US$75bn (calculated as a US$55bn increase in corporate long-
dated issuance versus a US$20bn increase in long-end Treasury
buybacks for 2026). In other words, long-dated issuance pressure
has been skewed towards corporates and away from US Treasuries.
We’d not consider this in any way destabilising. But it’s absolutely

a factor to take into account.

It’s always diﬃcult to put a basis point implication of this on long
dates, but it’s tough to argue that this is not a contributing factor.
Even with less corporate issuance outside the AI space for 2026,
aggregate corporate issuance is still well up, and with a longer
duration tilt to boot. These AI-spend players have also chosen to

issue bonds in place of bank borrowing in order to get the required
size and duration. The bank avenue would have meant banks'
funding is much shorter (traditional bank duration mismatch). Bank
lending is up, but nowhere near the same scale of the increase in

bond issuance. So, either way, back ends are feeling more pressure
than they would for long-dated funding.
That all being said, the market has remained quite receptive to AI
and wider corporate issuance. New issue premiums have tended to
be higher for longer duration / AI-related issuance, but in the 10bp
area they are far from tipping the long end over the edge.
The productivity piece pushes in the same
direction, but more mythical in nature
The productivity revolution that AI promises should enhance the

productive capacity of both capital and labour. It's actually diﬃcult
to glean this from observed outcomes, but theoretically,
productivity enhancement should correlate with upward pressure
on real yields. The theoretical chain here is from higher productivity
to higher expected return on capital to higher investment spending
ﬁnanced through higher issuance, in turn commanding higher real
rates. And in terms of the productivity of labour, it's diﬃcult to

argue that productivity is not signiﬁcantly enhanced for
knowledge-based activities and for customer support, and
especially in coding generally.
The dot.com boom, from the late 1990s into 2000, has echoes in
the AI one that we see today. The 10yr real yield was at 4% back
then. And that 4% real yield coincided with a 10yr Treasury yield at
comfortably over 6%. Arguably, the productivity story today is
even more persuasive than it was back then. The current 10yr real

yield is 2.9%. And the 10yr nominal yield is at 5.2%. So we're still at
least 100bp below the levels we saw back in 2000. Whether we like
it or not, rises in real yields for productivity reasons should be
construed as higher yields for 'positive' reasons.
A suggested breakout of inﬂuences from AI
Here's a suggested breakdown of the pure AI impact.
1. About 5% from the spending piece
2. About 25% from the issuance piece

3. About 70% from the productivity piece
But we can't leave it here, as AI is not the only inﬂuencer for long-
dated rates. In fact, we'd argue that two other drivers – inﬂation
and ﬁscal deﬁcits – have been more impactful.
It's far from all about the AI impact, as
holistically we have inﬂation and ﬁscal
deﬁcit factors too
It's tough to paint a picture of long rates without
incorporating the classic inﬂuences of inﬂation and ﬁscal
deﬁcits.
On the US ﬁscal deﬁcit issue speciﬁcally, the emphasis is on

the narrative and painting a picture of debt dynamics. Back
in 2024, we did a US special on this. See it here. ING's Carsten
Brzeski, James Knightley and James Smith recently penned a
wider update here. The ﬂy in the ointment here is that US
Treasury issuance is no higher this year than last, and the
same can be said of the ﬁscal deﬁcit in cash terms. Instead,
the market is concerned with the lack of US congressional
ambition to undertake measures to improve the path for

debt dynamics. Hence, the importance of the 'narrative' does
not improve for the foreseeable future.
The inﬂation proﬁle is also key. The pandemic shocked
developed markets back into inﬂation generation capability,
and the Iran war and energy price spikes have juiced things
up again. At the same time, where we are now for the US
long yields is no more than a return to slightly above normal,
by about 50bp in the 10yr. See more here on what that is all

about. Ahead, we have inﬂation remaining sticky to the
upside for 2026, before easing lower for 2027.
What's driving higher long-end yields?

2. Fiscal deﬁcit narrative = 30%
3. AI piece = 20%
Contain inﬂation, and we go a long way to containing
market rates. Deﬁcits mean supply, and that's an important
ongoing inﬂuencer. And AI? Well, it's more the 'good'
(productivity) than the 'bad' (issuance) that impacts. And the

ugly? Well, that's inﬂation. Even though break-even
expectations have remained contained, printed inﬂation has
not. For the second quarter, the US GDP deﬂator (eﬀectively
inﬂation) was 4.4%, and nominal GDP rose by 6.6%. The
current 10yr yield at 5.2% is not out of kilter with that. For
more on the prognosis ahead, see here and here.

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh is steering the Fed into a less transparent era. So, is that
a positive or negative development, and what could it mean for markets and the
economy? We speak with Former Fed governors Donald Kohn and Stephen Miran
as well as GS’ Jan Hatzius who agree that rigid “Odyssean” forward guidance that
commits policymakers to a pre-set path risks policy mistakes and is therefore rarely
appropriate. But they somewhat disagree about the benefits of great er transparency

around the Fed’s reaction function. Hatzius and Kohn argue that such transparency
helps markets better anticipate and price in policy changes, accelerating monetary
policy transmission. But Miran believes that the line between committing to a path
and being transparent is clearer in theory than in practice. And while GS economists
and strategists find that reduc ed transparency leads to increased market volatility,
the question is whether this just increases the market’s noise or also improves the

market’s signal. Hatzius argues largely for the former and Miran for the latter.
Jan Hatzius, Chief Economist and Head of Global Investment Research, Goldman Sachs
“Markets price what they think the Fed will do, not what they think the Fed should do. That will remain true even if the Fed
obscures its reaction function… market guesses about the Fed’s next moves will simply become worse, leading to more
volatility... and importantly, volatility that serves no constructive economic purpose.”

Donald Kohn, former Vice Chairman of the Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System, Robert V . Roosa
Chair in International Economics and Senior Fellow, Brookings Institution
“I sympathize with Chairman Warsh to some extent. [But] the real problem with Warsh’s approach is his reluctance to articulate
how the FOMC sees the economy evolving… explaining the Fed’s thinking about the economic outlook is essential in helping
markets act as a stabilizing force and is a core component of accountability.”
